TL;DR: SUFLECA is a weakly-supervised zero-shot CAD-to-image alignment method that learns geometry-aware features via large-scale NOC supervision and uses a geometrically consistent matching algorithm to achieve fast, accurate 9D pose estimation, outperforming zero-shot baselines and even some supervised methods on ScanNet25k.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address occlusion and sim-to-real domain shift in zero-shot CAD alignment by learning geometry-grounded representations that generalize across real and synthetic data; move beyond appearance-driven correspondences.

Method: Two main components: (1) scale up geometry-grounded feature learning by NOC supervision on 674K images across 12 datasets to learn compact, geometry-aware representations; (2) a geometrically consistent matching algorithm that yields reliable one-to-one CAD-to-image correspondences without iterative refinement.

Result: On ScanNet25k, SUFLECA achieves 33.4% category and 42.3% instance accuracy; outperforms the strongest zero-shot baseline by 10.3/12.2 percentage points; for the first time on this benchmark, surpasses fully supervised methods; runs in sub-second per object; code released at the provided URL.

Conclusion: Demonstrates that combining large-scale geometry-grounded representation learning with a geometrically consistent matching strategy yields robust, fast, zero-shot CAD alignment with strong cross-domain generalization, reducing reliance on iterative pose refinement.

Abstract: CAD-to-image alignment aims to estimate an object's 9D pose (rotation, translation, and anisotropic scale) from a single RGB image, enabling applications in robotics and augmented reality. Recent zero-shot methods use visual foundation models to match image regions to CAD models, yet typically their correspondences are appearance-driven and degrade under occlusion or sim-to-real domain shift. To address these limitations, we introduce SUFLECA (Scaling Up Feature LEarning for CAD Alignment), a weakly-supervised framework for zero-shot CAD alignment with two key contributions. First, SUFLECA scales up geometry-grounded feature learning from pretrained visual representations through Normalized Object Coordinates (NOCs) supervision on 674K images spanning 12 real and synthetic datasets, learning compact geometry-aware features that generalize across domains. Second, we propose a geometrically consistent matching algorithm that establishes reliable one-to-one CAD-to-image correspondences. Together, these contributions enable accurate, sub-second alignment per object instance without iterative pose refinement. On ScanNet25k, SUFLECA achieves 33.4%/42.3% category/instance accuracy, outperforming, with a smaller computational footprint, the strongest zero-shot baseline by 10.3/12.2 percentage points and, for the first time on this benchmark, even surpassing fully supervised methods. Code is available at: https://github.com/snt-arg/SUFLECA

</details>


### [53] [Blurring Modal Boundaries: A Unified Survey from Single- to Multi-Modal Person Re-ldentification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14821)
*Xiao Wang,Bing Wang,Bin Yang,Cuiqun Chen,Xin Xu,Mang Ye*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The abstract surveys cross-modal and multi-modal person re-identification (ReID), covering VI-ReID, TI-ReID, Sketch-ReID, NLOS-ReID, and multi-modal fusion, discusses datasets and challenges, and proposes a Transformer-based baseline for visible–infrared ReID with future research directions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome limitations of single-modal RGB ReID under adverse conditions (low light, occlusion) by leveraging cross-modal and non-line-of-sight modalities and sensor fusion to improve robustness and practicality in surveillance.

Method: Systematic survey of cross-modal and multi-modal ReID tasks; consolidation of datasets, challenges, and methods; design of a Transformer-based baseline framework for VI-ReID to learn modality-invariant representations.

Result: A Transformer-based baseline for VI-ReID is proposed, alongside a comprehensive synthesis of cross-modal ReID tasks, datasets, challenges, and methodological trends; the paper identifies performance gaps and directions for future research.

Conclusion: Cross-modal and multi-modal ReID is a rapidly advancing field. Transformer-based approaches offer a principled path to modality-invariant representations, and future work should emphasize richer datasets, robust cross-modal alignment, and exploration of tri-spectral/multi-modal fusion and NLOS scenarios.

Abstract: Person re-identification (ReID) serves as a critical component in intelligent surveillance systems, aiming to match identities across disjoint camera networks. While traditional methods primarily rely on single-modal RGB imagery, they are often constrained by environmental challenges such as low illumination and occlusion. To overcome these limitations, the field is rapidly evolving toward cross-modal and multi-modal paradigms. This survey presents a comprehensive overview of this transition, systematically reviewing key cross-modal tasks including visible-infrared (VI-ReID), text-image (TI-ReID), sketch-based (Sketch-ReID), and the emerging Non-Line-of-Sight (NLOS) ReID, which extends perception beyond direct visibility. Furthermore, we examine tri-spectral and multi-modal fusion ReID, discussing how complementary information from diverse sensors enhances robustness. Beyond summarizing datasets, challenges, and methodologies, we propose a Transformer-based baseline framework for visible-infrared ReID, designed to effectively capture modality-invariant features. Finally, based on the current landscape, we outline several promising directions for future research.

</details>


### [54] [Physics-Informed Diffusion for Biomechanically Plausible 3D Sign Language Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14836)
*Emanuele Colonna,Moises Diaz,Gennaro Vessio,Miguel Angel Ferrer,Giovanna Castellano*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A physics-informed diffusion model, PIDiffSign, translates glosses to pose for sign language by enforcing anatomical constraints via a differentiable geometry module and constraint losses, achieving improved pose realism and semantic fidelity over a diffusion baseline on PHOENIX14T and CSL-Daily.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Coordinate-based signatures of prior work allow biomechanical violations (bone-length drift, joint-angle violations, finger-locking) that degrade realism and semantic fidelity; a physics-informed approach can enforce anatomical plausibility during generation.

Method: Transformer encoder-decoder conditioned on diffusion time with adaptive zero-initialized layer normalization; cross-attentional gloss encoding; differentiable geometry module enforcing bone-length consistency and valid joint angles; training with anthropomorphic, kinematic, angular, and finger-joint constraints; contrastive gloss-pose alignment loss; classifier-free guidance for semantically conditioned sampling.

Result: Consistent improvements over a strong diffusion baseline in pose accuracy, joint-angle correctness, distributional realism, and back-translation quality on PHOENIX14T and CSL-Daily datasets.

Conclusion: Physics-informed diffusion enhances both motion realism and semantic fidelity in sign language generation by integrating anatomical constraints into architecture and training.

Abstract: Sign language production, which generates continuous 3D skeletal motion from spoken language input, must simultaneously satisfy two constraints: semantic fidelity, so that a deaf viewer can recognize the intended sequence of glosses, and biomechanical plausibility, so that the generated skeleton respects anatomical constraints. Existing approaches optimize semantic reconstruction through coordinate-based objectives that treat the skeleton as an unstructured vector, thus allowing for bone length drift, joint angle violations, and temporarily locked fingers. We introduce PIDiffSign, a physics-informed diffusion model for gloss-to-pose translation that incorporates anatomical constraints into both the architecture and training objective. The model uses a Transformer encoder-decoder, where the decoder is conditioned on the diffusion time step through adaptive zero-initialized layer normalization and cross-attends to gloss representations. A differentiable geometry module enforces bone length consistency and biologically valid joint angles throughout generation. Training combines anthropomorphic, kinematic, angular, and finger-joint constraints with a contrastive gloss-pose alignment loss and classifier-free guidance for semantically conditioned sampling. Experiments on the PHOENIX14T and CSL-Daily benchmarks show consistent improvements over a strong diffusion baseline in pose accuracy, joint-angle correctness, distributional realism, and back-translation quality. These results demonstrate that physics-informed diffusion improves both motion realism and semantic fidelity for sign language generation.

</details>


### [55] [Rotational Motion-Induced Error Compensation for Phase-Shifting Profilometry-Based Eye Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14876)
*Seong-Jin An,Sanghoon Jeon,Yatong An,Jae-Sang Hyun*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Rotational motion compensation improves PSP-based 3D eye reconstruction for dynamic eye tracking in VR/AR.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: PSP suffers from motion-induced phase errors during eye rotation; stable, high-precision 3D eye tracking is essential for immersive VR/AR systems.

Method: Estimate relative eye rotation from image-based cues using a user-specific 3D eye model in spherical coordinates; apply rotation-aware compensation for camera-pixel mismatch and phase-shift errors; use region-wise optimization to tailor compensation across ocular regions; validate with rotating fake eye under non-uniform motion and with a non-spherical rigid object.

Result: Significant suppression of motion-induced deformation and improved reconstruction accuracy; compensation works beyond spherical eye geometry, indicating broader applicability.

Conclusion: A practical framework enabling stable PSP-based dynamic 3D eye reconstruction, advancing high-precision eye tracking in immersive environments.

Abstract: With the proliferation of immersive Head-Mounted Displays (HMDs) for Virtual and Augmented Reality (VR/AR), reliable and high-precision eye tracking has become increasingly important. Conventional 2D image-based methods offer low system complexity but remain limited in stability, accuracy, and robustness. Three-dimensional ocular surface reconstruction can provide richer geomet-ric information, and structured light profilometry is particularly attractive because it enables dense and accurate surface measurement. However, Phase-Shifting Profilometry (PSP), which estimates phase from sequentially acquired fringe images, is highly susceptible to motion-induced errors when the eye rotates between frames. This study proposes a rotational motion compensation framework for PSP-based dynamic 3D eye reconstruction. Relative eye rotation is estimated from image-based motion cues using a user-specific 3D eye model in a spherical-coordinate domain. The estimated motion is then used to compensate for camera-pixel mismatch and phase-shift errors caused by inter-frame rotation. A region-wise optimization strategy is further introduced to reduce residual artifacts by inde-pendently refining the compensation strength in different ocular regions. Experiments with a rotating fake eye under non-uniform motion demonstrate that the proposed method substantially suppresses motion-induced deformation and improves reconstruction accuracy. An additional experiment with a non-spherical rigid object indicates that the compensation principle is not restricted to spherical eye geometry. These results establish a practical basis for stable PSP-based dynamic 3D eye reconstruction toward future high-precision eye tracking in immersive environments.

</details>


### [56] [Selectivity Drives Efficiency: Dataset Pruning for Visual Place Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14897)
*Tong Jin,Yunpeng Liu,Shuyu Hu,Chun Yuan,Song Wang,Feng Lu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Place-wise dataset pruning for VPR using intra-place diversity (IPD) and inter-place similarity (IPS) to create a compact coreset that preserves training efficacy, outperforming DP baselines with reduced data needs; strong results on MSLS-val and Nordland using NetVLAD.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address data inefficiency in VPR due to large-scale datasets and repeated training/evaluation; sample-wise DP overlooks pair-based supervision inherent to VPR.

Method: Introduce place-level pruning units; define IPD and IPS as metrics to assess training value of each place; rank places by joint IPD-IPS score; construct a coreset by selecting top-ranked places; demonstrate by pruning a merged dataset ~3.5x of GSV-Cities while maintaining performance; evaluate with NetVLAD; provide code release.

Result: Consistent outperforming of state-of-the-art DP baselines across pruning ratios; reduced selection/training costs; high performance retained on MSLS-val (94.5% R@1) and Nordland (97.0% R@1) with NetVLAD; confirms data efficiency without large loss in accuracy.

Conclusion: Placing the focus on places rather than images yields a more informative subset for VPR training; IPD and IPS are effective, complementary metrics for coreset construction; approach generalizes across datasets and reduces data/storage requirements; potential for broader adoption in VPR pipelines.

Abstract: Recent visual place recognition (VPR) studies have increasingly relied on large-scale datasets to train more robust and discriminative models. Although this trend significantly improves recognition performance, it also introduces substantial storage and training costs, especially when new architectures or training strategies need to be repeatedly developed and evaluated. Dataset pruning (DP) provides a promising way to improve data efficiency by retaining only informative training data. However, conventional DP methods mainly follow the sample-wise classification paradigm, which overlooks the relation-dependent training nature of VPR, where supervision is typically formed by image pairs rather than independent images. To address this issue, we propose a place-wise dataset pruning framework tailored for VPR. Instead of pruning individual images, our method treats each place as the basic pruning unit and introduces two complementary novel metrics, i.e., intra-place diversity (IPD) and inter-place similarity (IPS), to evaluate the training value of each place. By jointly considering these two metrics, our method ranks all places and constructs a compact yet informative coreset, thereby allowing the pruned dataset to still support the training of robust and discriminative VPR models. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method consistently outperforms state-of-the-art DP baselines under different pruning ratios while reducing selection and training costs. Moreover, by pruning a merged dataset roughly 3.5$\times$ the size of GSV-Cities to a comparable scale, our coreset maintains highly competitive performance, achieving 94.5\% R@1 on MSLS-val and 97.0\% R@1 on Nordland with only NetVLAD. Codes will be made publicly available.

</details>


### [57] [FlashDecoder: Real-Time Latent-to-Pixel Streaming Decoder with Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14898)
*Minguk Kang,Suha Kwak*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A fast, memory-efficient pure-Transformer decoder (FlashDecoder) for latent video diffusion that decodes frames sequentially with a fixed-size rolling window, enabling constant-latency streaming while matching convolutional decoders in quality and delivering 3.6×–4.7× speedups with far less memory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current latent video diffusion decoders rely on slow, memory-intensive 3D convolutions at high resolutions/long horizons; need fast, memory-bounded decoders for real-time streaming.

Method: A sequential, pure-Transformer video decoder that processes frames one by one. Each frame attends only to a fixed-size window of past frames via a rolling KV cache, enforcing temporal causality without explicit masks. Training accommodates resolutions up to 1080p. Architecture-aware inference optimizations further boost speed.

Result: On Wan2.1 and Wan2.2 latent spaces, FlashDecoder matches each convolutional decoder in PSNR (e.g., 41.55 dB vs. 41.49 dB at 1080p) while decoding 3.6×–4.7× faster with up to 11× less memory on a single H100 GPU. With additional optimizations, speedups reach ~12×.

Conclusion: FlashDecoder delivers real-time capable, memory-efficient decoding for latent video diffusion, maintaining reconstruction quality while substantially reducing latency and memory, enabling constant-latency streaming at 1080p.

Abstract: Real-time video generation demands fast decoding as much as fast denoising, yet current latent video diffusion models rely on 3D convolutional decoders that are slow and memory-intensive at high resolutions or for long video. We introduce FlashDecoder, a fast, memory-efficient pure-Transformer video decoder that decodes latents to pixels frame by frame. At each step, the current frame attends only to a fixed-size window of past frames through a rolling KV cache. The fixed temporal window keeps decoding fast and memory bounded regardless of video length, enabling constant-latency streaming. Because frames are processed sequentially, temporal causality is enforced without explicit attention masks, enabling training at resolutions up to 1080p and matching the reconstruction quality of convolutional decoders. On the Wan2.1 and Wan2.2 latent spaces, FlashDecoder matches each convolutional decoder in reconstruction quality (e.g., 41.55dB vs. 41.49dB PSNR at 1080p) while decoding 3.6x-4.7x faster with up to 11x less memory on a single H100 GPU. With architecture-aware inference optimizations, the speedup widens to 12x.

</details>


### [58] [TanGO: Training-Free 3D Editing via Tangent-Space Guidance and Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14927)
*Siwoo Lim,Sunjae Yoon,Gwanhyeong Koo,Hyeonseo Yun,Chang D. Yoo*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TanGO enables per-token, training-free editing in 3D flow-matching models by applying adaptive tangent-space control with a one-step optimal control rule and a von Mises–Fisher-inspired discrepancy to set per-token strengths, reducing artifacts and achieving state-of-the-art edits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome semantic artifacts (collapsed preserved regions, incomplete transformations) caused by shared global context in tokens of structured 3D generative models during editing, enabling more precise, local edits without training.

Method: Introduce a one-step optimal control framework in the tangent space of generative dynamics for per-token steering. Compute token-wise control strength from a von Mises–Fisher-inspired directional discrepancy between source and target velocity fields, enabling adaptive, selective manipulation without training.

Result: Empirical results show substantial reduction of structural artifacts and superior editing performance compared with existing 3D editing baselines, achieving state-of-the-art results on relevant benchmarks; the authors release code.

Conclusion: TanGO provides a training-free, adaptive editing mechanism in the tangent space of generative dynamics for structured 3D models, enabling faithful local edits with fewer artifacts.

Abstract: While recent flow-matching 3D generative models (e.g., VecSet) adopt structured representations, their tokens share global context, causing conventional training-free editing to suffer from semantic artifacts such as collapsed preserved regions or incomplete transformations. To address this, we propose TanGO, a training-free framework that enables adaptive per-token steering in the tangent space of generative dynamics. To realize this selective control, we formulate a one-step optimal control rule and determine the strength of each token's control signal using a von Mises-Fisher inspired directional discrepancy derived from the source and target velocity fields. Experiments show that TanGO substantially reduces structural artifacts and achieves state-of-the-art performance, outperforming existing 3D editing baselines. The code is publicly available at https://github.com/siw00-lim/TanGO.

</details>


### [59] [Benchmarking Face Recognition without Real Faces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14932)
*Paweł Borsukiewicz,Daniele Lunghi,Wendkûuni C. Ouédraogo,Jacques Klein,Tegawendé F. Bissyandé*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Synthetic face datasets can rival real benchmarks for evaluating face recognition; MorphFace and Vec2Face most faithfully reproduce real-benchmark behavior across models and metrics, enabling privacy-preserving benchmarking.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether synthetic datasets can replace real-face benchmarks for evaluating face recognition systems, addressing privacy and data collection concerns.

Method: Comparative evaluation of 12 synthetic datasets vs 7 established real benchmarks using 24 pre-trained models spanning CNNs and transformers. Assessed biometric verification metrics, score distributions, cross-model ranking consistency, and underlying distributional properties.

Result: Benchmark fidelity varied across synthetic datasets; MorphFace and Vec2Face best reproduce relative real-benchmark behavior and agree within the natural disagreement observed among real benchmarks themselves.

Conclusion: Well-constructed synthetic datasets can support reliable comparative evaluation for face recognition, moving toward a fully synthetic, privacy-preserving pipeline for training and benchmarking.

Abstract: Synthetic face datasets have become effective enough to train face recognition models with accuracy rivaling that of models trained on real photographs. This progress sidesteps the ethical and legal burdens of collecting real biometric data, yet evaluation has not kept pace. Even studies that train entirely on synthetic images still rely on real-face benchmarks to measure performance, leaving the privacy problem only half solved. We ask whether synthetic datasets can replace real benchmarks for face recognition evaluation. We test 12 synthetic datasets against 7 established real benchmarks using 24 pre-trained models that span both convolutional and transformer architectures. Our evaluation covers biometric verification metrics, similarity score distributions, cross-model ranking consistency, and the underlying distributional properties of each dataset. Benchmarking fidelity varies widely across the synthetic candidates, but the two strongest, MorphFace and Vec2Face, reproduce the relative behavior of real benchmarks and reach agreement levels that fall within the natural disagreement already observed among the real benchmarks themselves. These results establish that well-constructed synthetic datasets can support reliable comparative evaluation for face recognition, moving the field closer to a fully synthetic and privacy-preserving pipeline for both training and benchmarking.

</details>


### [60] [Still image and spatial-temporal tomato data enabling detection, segmentation, tracking, and video-instance segmentation using strong and weak labels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14934)
*Michael Halstead,Esra Guclu,Mohamed Farag,Enrico Pallotta,Christian Hund,Ribana Roscher,Maren Bennewitz,Juergen Gall,Cyrill Stachniss,Chris McCool*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Two real-world tomato datasets for robotic sensing: BUTom21 (still images with manual pixel-level ripeness labels) and BUTom-ST21 (video with AI-generated pseudo-labels), both enabling per-fruit ripeness labeling and temporal fruit counting for field phenotyping.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address the need for realistic, field-scale data to develop robust vision-based sensing and estimation of tomato fruit ripeness in commercial-like environments; enable pixel-accurate labeling and temporal analysis for per-fruit counting.

Method: Data collected with a robot in commercial-like settings. BUTom21 consists of still images with manual pixel-level annotations of fruit ripeness. BUTom-ST21 consists of video sequences with semi-automated annotations via AI-based pseudo-labels. Both datasets include per-fruit ripeness labels; the video dataset provides spatial-temporal data enabling fruit counting over time.

Result: Release of two datasets (BUTom21 and BUTom-ST21) with pixel-level ripeness labels; BUTom-ST21 adds temporal data and pseudo-labels; facilitates evaluation of perception methods in challenging real-world imagery and per-fruit counting for field phenotyping.

Conclusion: These datasets provide a valuable benchmark resource for sensing, ripeness estimation, and fruit counting in realistic greenhouse-like settings, promoting advances in field-based tomato phenotyping and robotic harvesting research.

Abstract: In this manuscript we release two datasets for visual sensing of tomato plants grown in commercial-like settings and acquired using a robot. The first is BUTom21 which consists of still images and manual annotations. The second is BUTom-ST21 which consists of video-based data and semi-automated annotations through AI-based methods, referred to as pseudo-labels. In both cases, we provide pixel-level labels for the ripeness of the fruit. The aim is to provide the research community a challenging set of real-world imagery to explore methods to sense and estimate the state of tomato plants and their fruit, which is an important horticultural crop. Importantly, the spatial-temporal dataset provides individual fruit count and ripeness information enabling researchers to push the boundaries of field-based phenotyping.

</details>


### [61] [VideoChat3: Fully Open Video MLLM for Efficient and Generalist Video Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14935)
*Xinhao Li,Yuhan Zhu,Xiangyu Zeng,Yuhao Dong,Haoning Wu,Zhiqiu Zhang,Yuandong Yang,Changlian Ma,Qingyu Zhang,Yansong Shi,Xinyu Chen,Haoran Chen,Zizheng Huang,Jun Zhang,Kun Ouyang,Lin Sui,Ziang Yan,Yicheng Xu,Chenting Wang,Yinan He,Hongjie Zhang,Yi Wang,Yu Qiao,Yali Wang,Ziwei Liu,Kai Chen,Limin Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: VideoChat3 is an open, efficient, generalist video-centric MLLM that generalizes across domains with 4B parameters, using I3D-ViT and adaptive frame resolution for efficiency, plus a scalable multi-dataset synthesis pipeline including three datasets; it outperforms prior open-source models on general, long-form, and streaming benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing open-source video models struggle to generalize across diverse video domains, are computationally heavy, and lack full openness (training code, strategies, or datasets). There is a need for a broadly applicable, efficient, and fully open video MLLM.

Method: Twofold design: efficiency via Inflated 3D Vision Transformer (I3D-ViT) and Adaptive Frame Resolution for Streaming Video Perception; effectiveness via scalable video data synthesis pipeline creating three datasets: VideoChat3-Academic2M, VideoChat3-LV116K, VideoChat3-OL617K; training with 4B parameters.

Result: Empirical results on general, long-form, and streaming benchmarks show VideoChat3 surpasses prior open-source models with comparable or larger parameters, achieving higher efficiency.

Conclusion: VideoChat3 demonstrates a strong generalization-efficiency trade-off, is fully open, and supports reproducibility and community-driven development in video-language modeling.

Abstract: Recent advances in video understanding have spanned motion, long video, and streaming interaction, driving this field toward real-world applications. Despite this progress, current open-source models remain limited in several ways. They often struggle to generalize across diverse video types, making them effective only in specific domains. High computational demands further restrict their efficiency and scalability. Moreover, most models are only partially open, with key components such as training code, strategy, or datasets unavailable, which hinders reproducibility and slows community-driven development. To address these issues, we introduce VideoChat3, a fully open, efficient, and generalist video-centric MLLM. VideoChat3 advances video understanding through two complementary designs. For efficiency, we introduce Inflated 3D Vision Transformer (I3D-ViT) and Adaptive Frame Resolution for Streaming Video Perception, which enables efficient spatiotemporal representation and reduces the cost of processing video inputs during training and inference. For effectiveness, we develop a scalable video data synthesis pipeline that curates three diverse, high-quality training datasets: VideoChat3-Academic2M, VideoChat3-LV116K, and VideoChat3-OL617K, covering general, long-form, and streaming video scenarios, improving the model's generalization across domains. By integrating these designs, VideoChat3 achieves a rare balance of broad generalization and computational efficiency. Experiments across general, long-form, and streaming benchmarks demonstrate that VideoChat3 surpasses prior open-source models with equal or larger parameter counts with only 4B parameters and higher efficiency.

</details>


### [62] [Frequency-Structured Field Learning for Light-Field Disparity Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14941)
*Sara Monji-Azad,Yulin Liu,Jürgen Hesser*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Fourier-local latent-field learning (FreqLF) for light-field disparity that avoids explicit cost volumes by updating EPI-derived latent features through stacked Fourier-local layers and a Gaussian-mixture decoder, achieving competitive accuracy on the HCI 4D benchmark.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Light-field disparity requires global consistency in smooth regions and local precision near occlusions; existing methods rely on cost volumes, EPI matching, or regression with limited global coupling and high memory costs. A field-level approach could unify global and local cues more efficiently.

Method: Encode angular parallax cues from horizontal/vertical EPIs and central-view appearance into a latent field. Update the latent field with stacked hybrid Fourier-local layers combining Fourier low-mode global interactions and local convolutions to preserve detail. A coordinate-conditioned Gaussian-mixture decoder predicts disparity; the mixture mean is used as final estimate.

Result: On the HCI 4D Light Field Benchmark, FreqLF matches strong supervised baselines in accuracy while avoiding explicit cost-volume construction. Ablations show the Fourier and local branches provide complementary gains. Scaling experiments demonstrate robustness across spatial resolutions.

Conclusion: Fourier-local latent-field learning is a competitive alternative for light-field disparity estimation, offering global–local integration without explicit disparity volumes. Code will be released.

Abstract: Light-field disparity estimation requires global consistency in smooth or textureless regions and local precision near occlusion boundaries, thin structures, and abrupt depth transitions. Existing methods address these requirements through EPI matching, cost-volume or focal-stack construction, view aggregation, or direct convolutional regression, often relying on local windows, discrete disparity hypotheses, memory-intensive volumes, or attention-based aggregation. We instead formulate disparity estimation at the field level, predicting disparity from globally and locally updated EPI-derived latent features without explicitly constructing a disparity volume. We introduce FreqLF, an EPI-guided Fourier-local framework that encodes angular parallax cues from horizontal and vertical EPI stacks together with central-view appearance features. These cues are projected into a latent field and updated through stacked hybrid Fourier-local layers. Fourier low-mode updates enable global feature interaction, while local convolutions preserve spatial variations needed for fine disparity detail. A coordinate-conditioned Gaussian-mixture decoder then predicts disparity, using the mixture mean as the final estimate. Experiments on the HCI 4D Light Field Benchmark show that FreqLF approaches the accuracy of strong supervised baselines while avoiding explicit cost-volume construction in the base model. Ablations confirm the complementary roles of the Fourier and local branches, and scaling experiments demonstrate practical behavior across spatial resolutions. These results suggest that Fourier-local latent field learning is a competitive alternative for light-field disparity estimation. The code will be published soon.

</details>


### [63] [Introspective Attention Modulation for Safe Text-to-Image Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14945)
*Basim Azam,Hossein Rahmani,Naveed Akhtar*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Proposes an inference-time attention introspection mechanism to regulate diffusion-transformer image generation for safety, rebalancing attention activations to avoid unsafe concepts while preserving semantic alignment; claims robustness against adversarial prompts and improved safety/perceptual quality; code at basim-azam.github.io/iam/


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: State-of-the-art flow-based T2I models generate unsafe content; existing safety approaches (concept erasure, prompt filtering, classifier gating) are easily bypassed by simple adaptations; need an intrinsic, robust safety mechanism.

Method: Inference-time introspection that analyzes and rebalances attention activations during image synthesis to steer away from unsafe concepts while maintaining semantic alignment; regulates attention-space dynamically without retraining; operates within diffusion-transformer framework to enforce safety.

Result: On standard and adversarial safety benchmarks, achieves high safety scores while preserving or improving alignment and perceptual quality; attention-space regulation outperforms concept erasure; code release available for reproducibility.

Conclusion: Attention-space regulation via inference-time attention dynamics offers a promising, intrinsic path to safer diffusion-based image generation, potentially superior to erasure-based methods; supports safety without sacrificing quality.

Abstract: State-of-the-art flow based text-to-image (T2I) models exhibit remarkable generative abilities but remain vulnerable to producing unsafe content. Prior safety efforts range from concept erasure and prompt filtering to classifier-based gating. However, simple techniques like parameter efficient adaptations of the models easily bypass such guardrails. We introduce a unique principled approach that achieves safety by regulating the model's attention dynamics through inference-time introspection, exhibiting intrinsic robustness. Our method analyzes and rebalances attention activations throughout image synthesis, steering generations away from unsafe concepts while preserving semantic alignment. This introspective control ensures safety of deployed models. Across standard and adversarial safety benchmarks, our approach achieves remarkable safety scores while maintaining or even improving alignment and perceptual quality. Our results reveal that attention-space regulation offers a considerably more promising path to safer diffusion transformer based image generation than the existing concept erasing mechanism.Our code can be accessed at https://basim-azam.github.io/iam/

</details>


### [64] [DINE: Distance Is Not Enough -- Learning Global Deformation Priors for Robust Soft-Tissue Point Cloud Registration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14946)
*Sara Monji-Azad,Rohit Beer,Marvin Kinz,Claudia Scherl,Jürgen Hesser*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: DINE adds a learned prior over displacement fields to non-rigid point cloud registration, improving global deformation plausibility and robustness over Chamfer-based methods; it uses two priors (PCA Gaussian and per-vector normalizing-flow) in a two-stage training pipeline, yielding substantial gains on DeformedTissue and SynBench.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Chamfer-based objectives promote local point-wise proximity but do not enforce global plausibility of the deformation field, making performance degrade under large deformations, noise, and outliers in soft-tissue analysis.

Method: Two-stage MAP framework (DINE) applied to Backbone models Robust-DefReg and DefTransNet. Stage 1 trains with Chamfer distance; Stage 2 uses predicted Stage-1 deformations to estimate a prior and refines with a combined distance plus negative log-prior objective. Compare a full-field PCA Gaussian prior versus a per-vector normalizing-flow prior.

Result: On DeformedTissue, DINE-PCA reduces mean Chamfer distance by ~27–69% relative to Stage-1 across deformation levels and boosts robustness by up to 66% for outliers and 83% for Gaussian noise. On SynBench, improvements are modest at the smallest deformation levels but reach ~59–79% from moderate to severe deformation.

Conclusion: Global deformation plausibility is an important constraint for reliable soft-tissue non-rigid registration; the two-stage MAP approach with learned priors (PCA Gaussian or per-vector NF) improves registration robustness and accuracy; code will be released.

Abstract: Non-rigid point cloud registration is central to soft-tissue shape analysis, but large deformations, noise, and outliers make correspondence estimation challenging. Most learning-based methods rely on local objectives such as Chamfer distance, which encourage point-wise proximity but do not constrain the global plausibility of the predicted deformation field. We address this limitation with DINE, a maximum a posteriori framework that augments distance-based registration with a learned statistical prior over displacement vector fields. DINE is applied to two registration backbones, Robust-DefReg and DefTransNet, using a two-stage strategy: a first-stage model is trained with Chamfer distance, its predicted deformation fields are used to estimate a prior, and the model is then refined with a combined distance and negative log-prior objective. We compare a full-field PCA Gaussian prior with a per-vector normalizing-flow prior. Experiments on DeformedTissue and SynBench show lower mean Chamfer distance under deformation and corruption. On DeformedTissue, DINE-PCA reduces Chamfer distance by approximately 27--69\% relative to the corresponding Stage-1 backbone across deformation levels, and improves robustness by up to 66\% for outliers and 83\% for Gaussian noise. On SynBench, improvements are modest at the smallest deformation levels and reach approximately 59--79\% from moderate to severe deformation. These results suggest that global deformation plausibility is an important constraint for reliable soft-tissue point cloud registration. (The code will be published soon.)

</details>


### [65] [U-shaped Multi-granularity Learning for Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14966)
*Biao Chen,Yunqian Yu,Xiangxu Zhao,Zhongshu Chen,Mengmeng Jing,Lin Zuo*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A U-shaped multi-granularity prompt-learning framework (UPrompt) for vision-language models that unifies coarse and fine representations in both visual and textual modalities via a U-Net–style encoder–decoder with cross-level connections, achieving strong gains on dense prediction tasks with low extra overhead.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the granularity dilemma in prompt learning for vision-language models: global prompts miss fine-grained semantic detail, while local prompts miss contextual relationships, hindering cross-task generalization in dense prediction tasks.

Method: Define parallel multi-granularity representations for both visual and textual streams. Use coarse-to-fine cascaded enhancement to propagate global context and refine local details, along with fine-to-coarse hierarchical supervision to enforce semantic consistency across scales. Employ a U-shaped encoder-decoder style architecture with cross-level connections to unify multi-scale prompts across modalities.

Result: Empirical validation on 17 benchmarks shows substantial gains: UPrompt surpasses MAMET and VPKE by 4.1 and 7.3 rSum on MSCOCO; exceeds CoCoA-Mix by 5.09% in base-to-novel generalization; demonstrates minimal overhead for coarse-grained prompts and matches PSRC with one-third cost for medium-grained prompts.

Conclusion: UPrompt effectively blends multi-granularity prompts across vision and language, improving cross-task generalization in dense prediction settings while maintaining competitive efficiency.

Abstract: The prompt learning paradigm for vision-language models is effective yet faces a granularity dilemma: global prompts lack fine-grained semantic awareness, while local prompts ignore contextual associations, limiting cross-task generalization. This dilemma exists in dense prediction tasks. Inspired by U-Net, which unifies multi-level representations across granularities, we propose UPrompt, a U-shaped multi-granularity prompt learning framework for vision-language models. Similar to how U-Net integrates fine and coarse features through symmetric encoder-decoder pathways with cross-level connections, UPrompt constructs parallel multi-granularity representations in both visual and textual modalities, where coarse-to-fine cascaded enhancement propagates global context to refine local details, while fine-to-coarse hierarchical supervision ensures semantic consistency across scales. Extensive experiments on 17 benchmarks validate our effectiveness. UPrompt outperforms MAMET and VPKE by 4.1 and 7.3 rSum on MSCOCO, surpasses CoCoA-Mix by 5.09% in base-to-novel generalization, while maintaining competitive performance with minimal overhead (coarse-grained) and matching PSRC with 1/3 cost (medium-grained).

</details>


### [66] [Stitch-Inferencer: Enhance Endoscopic Video Segmentation and Tracking via Panoramic Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14968)
*Shunsuke Kikuchi,Atsushi Kouno,Hiroki Matsuzaki*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A real-time, model-agnostic framework (Stitch-Inferencer) uses an explicit image-space panoramic canvas to stitch observations across frames, expanding effective field-of-view and enabling long-range context for endoscopic perception without retraining; achieves real-time throughput (~60 FPS stitching) and improves segmentation/tracking.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Endoscopic perception suffers from limited field-of-view and frequent occlusions; existing video models rely on implicit memory, require task-specific training, large annotated data, and high computation. A memory mechanism that preserves observed pixels explicitly could provide robust, real-time context.

Method: Construct an online panoramic canvas by stitching valid observations from successive frames, creating an explicit memory of pixels; downstream segmentation or tracking models operate on a compact ROI in the panorama, and predictions are reprojected to the current frame; the framework is model-agnostic and real-time.

Result: Empirical evaluation on anatomy segmentation and point/box tracking shows consistent improvements across diverse baselines while maintaining real-time throughput; the stitching module achieves >60 FPS; source code to be released.

Conclusion: Explicit image-space memory via panoramic stitching provides long-range context for endoscopic perception without retraining, enabling efficient, instrument-free, real-time inference and broad applicability to related video-perception tasks.

Abstract: Surgical video understanding is fundamental to navigation systems. Endoscopic perception is often hindered by a limited field-of-view and frequent instrument occlusions, making spatio-temporal context essential for robust inference. These challenges have motivated video models that aggregate information across frames. However, existing video models typically store past observations implicitly in learned feature representations, often requiring task-specific video training, substantial annotated data, and increased computational cost. We propose Stitch-Inferencer, a real-time, model-agnostic inference framework that replaces implicit feature memory with an explicit image-space panoramic canvas. By stitching valid observations across frames, Stitch-Inferencer preserves previously observed pixels in an online, instrument-free view, expanding the effective field-of-view and providing direct access to regions that are temporarily occluded or absent from the current frame. Downstream segmentation or tracking models are applied to a compact region of interest on the panorama, and their predictions are reprojected to the current frame, enabling existing models to exploit long-range context without retraining. Experiments on anatomy segmentation and point/box tracking demonstrate consistent improvements across diverse baselines while preserving real-time throughput. The stitching module alone runs at over 60 FPS, providing a practical inference-time solution to enhance endoscopic perception in computationally constrained intraoperative environments. Source code will be made publicly available.

</details>


### [67] [On Success and Simplicity: A Second Look at Transferable Vision-Language Attack Pipeline](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14974)
*Yuchen Ren,Zhengyu Zhao,Chenhao Lin,Bo Yang,Chao Shen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SimVLA proposes a simple, efficient vision-language attack against VLPMs that outperforms complex, multi-stage pipelines by addressing three overlooked cross-modal interaction issues. It achieves higher transferability (e.g., Flickr30k R@1 +8.01% to +14.71%), with substantially lower runtime and memory usage, suggesting that domain knowledge (like cross-modal word identification) is more beneficial than intricate loss functions and multiple stages.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: VLPM attacks in prior work rely on complex, multi-stage pipelines and elaborate loss functions. The authors argue that inappropriate cross-modal interactions and excessive operations hurt transferability and efficiency, and aim to demonstrate that a simpler, knowledge-informed attack can be more effective and scalable.

Method: Introduce SimVLA (Simple Vision-Language Attack). Identify three ignored issues caused by cross-modal interactions and over-operations. Leverage domain knowledge (e.g., cross-modal word identification) to craft a streamlined attack pipeline with fewer components and stages. Evaluate on four datasets and three downstream tasks; compare transferability and efficiency against state-of-the-art baselines. Provide code at the referenced GitHub repository.

Result: Empirical results show improved transferability and efficiency. On Flickr30k text–image retrieval, SimVLA surpasses SOTA in R@1 transferability by 8.01%–14.71%, while consuming ~35.73% of the runtime and ~46.26% of peak VRAM. Across four datasets and three downstream tasks, SimVLA demonstrates superior performance and simplicity compared to complex attack pipelines.

Conclusion: Blindly pursuing intricate loss functions and multi-stage designs can be counterproductive. Incorporating domain knowledge to address cross-modal interactions yields a simple yet effective backbone for transferable attacks on VLPMs, with broad applicability to future extensions.

Abstract: Vision-Language Pre-training Models (VLPMs) are known to be vulnerable to adversarial attacks. Recent transferable attacks on VLPMs have followed a common pipeline with complicated loss functions or multi-stage text/image attacks. However, in this paper, we demonstrate that such a sophisticated attack pipeline can be simpler yet more successful. Specifically, we identify three previously overlooked issues caused by inappropriate cross-modal interactions and excessive operations. To address them, we propose the Simple Vision-Language Attack (SimVLA) pipeline, which observably improves transferability and efficiency. Experiments on four datasets and three downstream tasks validate the superiority of our pipeline. For instance, on Flickr30k text-image retrieval dataset, our SimVLA outperforms the SOTA baseline in R@1 transferability by 8.01\%-14.71\%, while consuming only about 35.73\% of the time and 46.26\% of the max VRAM. Overall, the superiority of our SimVLA highlights the importance of leveraging domain knowledge (e.g., our proposed cross-modal word identification), while blindly pursuing intricate operations (e.g, complex loss functions and redundant multi-stage designs) may even be harmful. We hope our SimVLA can serve as a simple yet effective backbone for future extensions. Code is available at https://github.com/RYC-98/SimVLA.

</details>


### [68] [From Draft to Draft-Free: One-Step Video Object Removal via Privileged Distillation and Fast Planting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14976)
*Zizhao Chen,Ping Wei,Guang Dai,Jingdong Wang,Mengmeng Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: One-shot video object removal (D2DF) distills multi-step draft refinement into a single-step model; draft-conditioned and draft-free variants use PPCD and SGFP, achieving state-of-the-art quality and ~1s per video.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing methods either introduce artifacts (optical flow/attention-based approaches) or are computationally expensive due to diffusion-based multi-step denoising. A one-step model that leverages drafts or autonomous pseudo-drafts can improve realism and efficiency.

Method: Train a teacher to iteratively refine low-quality removal drafts into high-fidelity videos. Distill this capability into a one-step student via Prior-Privileged Consistency Distillation (PPCD) conditioned on the draft. Introduce Self-Guided Fast Planting (SGFP) with a Temporal Masked Transformer to autonomously generate scene-consistent pseudo-drafts in latent space, yielding a fully draft-free one-step model.

Result: Draft-conditioned and draft-free D2DF variants achieve state-of-the-art performance across multiple metrics, surpassing traditional and multi-step diffusion-based methods in both quality and efficiency. Denoising per video is reported to take ~1 second.

Conclusion: D2DF provides a practical, high-fidelity one-step video removal solution. PPCD enables high-quality draft-conditioned learning, and SGFP enables a fully draft-free model, combining speed and realism suitable for real-time or near-real-time video editing.

Abstract: Video object removal is a fundamental yet challenging task in video editing. Despite recent progress, existing methods typically fall into two categories. Traditional approaches based on optical flow or attention mechanisms often introduce noticeable artifacts and yield unnatural results. In contrast, diffusion-based methods improve visual realism but demand multiple denoising steps, limiting their practicality. To address these issues, we propose From-Draft-to-Draft-Free (D2DF), a framework that distills the ability of transforming coarse drafts into refined videos into a one-step video generation model. Within D2DF, a teacher model is trained to refine low-quality removal results ("drafts") into high-fidelity videos by multiple steps. Then, through Prior-Privileged Consistency Distillation (PPCD), we distill this capability into a student model that performs one-step removal conditioned on the draft. To eliminate draft dependency, we introduce a Self-Guided Fast Planting (SGFP) module based on our Temporal Masked Transformer that autonomously generates scene-consistent pseudo-drafts in latent space, enabling a fully draft-free one-step model. Extensive experiments show that both draft-conditioned and draft-free versions achieve state-of-the-art performance on multiple metrics, surpassing traditional and multi-step generative methods in both quality and efficiency. The denoising process for a single video takes only about 1 second.

</details>


### [69] [JADE-GS: Joint Alternating Deblurring Guided by Events in 3D Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14990)
*Haoyu Fu,Jiafeng Huang,Yuchen Wang,Shengjie Zhao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: JADE-GS proposes a pixel-adaptive fusion of physics-based event priors and learned restoration, coupled in a bidirectional 2D-3D loop with Gaussian Splatting to produce geometry-aware, real-time capable 3D supervision from event cameras.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the opposing failure modes of event-based priors (edge-preserving but drift-prone) and learned restoration (texture-faithful but boundary-distorting), and to address uncorrected event noise and biases from one-way pipelines that rely on fixed pseudo-labels for geometry.

Method: Introduce JADE-GS: a pixel-adaptive routing gate that fuses complementary priors, a 2D restorer coupled to a 3D Gaussian Splatting student in a bidirectional loop, with detached, multi-view-consistent renders and a physics-based reblurring constraint to regularize the restorer, turning a fixed preprocessor into a geometry-aware predictor.

Result: On synthetic and real benchmarks, achieves best perceptual quality (LPIPS, CLIP-IQA) on both benchmarks, with competitive PSNR and SSIM; trains in ~1 hour on a single consumer GPU with <5 GB, while preserving real-time rendering.

Conclusion: JADE-GS effectively combines complementary 2D priors and a geometry-aware 3D supervision loop to deliver state-of-the-art perceptual quality for event-based 3D reconstruction, with efficient training enabling real-time performance.

Abstract: When a camera moves fast during exposure, blur destroys the intra-exposure motion a 3D model needs to recover the sharp scene, while event cameras capture exactly this signal at microsecond resolution. Turning them into reliable 3D supervision faces two obstacles. First, the two restoration priors fail in opposite ways: physics-based event-integration priors preserve edges but accumulate drift; learned networks recover texture but distort boundaries. Second, existing pipelines run in one direction only, so raw event noise or the biases of fixed 2D pseudo-labels pass uncorrected into the geometry. JADE-GS addresses both: a pixel-adaptive routing gate fuses the complementary priors, and the resulting 2D restorer is coupled to a 3D Gaussian Splatting student in a bidirectional loop, where detached, multi-view-consistent renders and a physics-based reblurring constraint regularize the restorer, turning a fixed preprocessor into a geometry-aware predictor. Across synthetic and real benchmarks, JADE-GS attains the best perceptual quality, leading LPIPS and CLIP-IQA on both benchmarks with competitive PSNR and SSIM, and trainsin about one hour under 5 GB on a single consumer GPU while preserving real-time rendering.

</details>


### [70] [Video = World + Event Stream](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15038)
*Lianghua Huang,Zhi-Fan Wu,Yupeng Shi,Wei Wang,Mengyang Feng,Cheng Yu,Chen Liang,Junjie He,Chen-Wei Xie,Yu Liu,Jingren Zhou,Ang Wang,Bang Zhang,Baole Ai,Chongyang Zhong,Jinwei Qi,Kai Zhu,Pandeng Li,Peng Zhang,Wenyuan Zhang,Xinhua Cheng,Yitong Huang,Yun Zheng,Yuxiang Bao,Yuzheng Wang,Zhiwei Lin,Zoubin Bi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Wan-Streamer v0.3 reframes real-time video understanding as a 'world plus event stream' model, enabling a general pretraining task that supports real-time multimodal interactions and downstream tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address the need for real-time, immersive video intelligence by unifying persistent world context with time-varying event streams to enable generalizable pretraining for downstream real-time AV tasks.

Method: Define a two-component input—world (persistent context) and event stream (time-varying changes). Train on large real-video data to predict world dynamics given incoming input. Instantiate on real-time full-duplex audio-visual interaction; multimodal pipeline maps user input to language-form speech and behavioral actions; maintain existing operating point (640x368, 25 FPS, 160 ms streaming unit, ~200 ms model latency, ~550 ms total latency with 350 ms network budget).

Result: Describes architecture and operating metrics rather than a traditional empirical evaluation. Demonstrates compatibility with a real-time, low-latency AV loop and specifies precise latency/throughput targets.

Conclusion: A general-purpose pretraining framework for real-time AV interaction that can be specialized to a broad class of downstream tasks, preserving established performance characteristics while expanding real-time capabilities.

Abstract: We present Wan-Streamer v0.3, which reframes our native-streaming interaction model under a single organizing view: a video is a world plus an event stream. The world is the persistent context in which a video unfolds, including the environment, scene, subjects, ambient acoustic conditions, voice characteristics, and other relatively stable conditions. The event stream is everything that changes over time within that world, including scene or environmental changes, subject behavior, speech, and other sounds. This yields a general-purpose pretraining task over large amounts of real video: given a world and incoming input, predict how the world moves, changes, and responds in real time. The resulting competence can be specialized to a broad family of real-time downstream tasks. We instantiate it on real-time full-duplex audio-visual interaction, where the event stream is the agent's speech together with free-form behavior. Functionally, the model's multimodal understanding process is vision-language-action-like: it maps multimodal user input to language-form speech and behavior actions. Wan-Streamer v0.3 preserves the v0.2 operating point: 640x368 video at 25 FPS, a 160 ms streaming unit, approximately 200 ms model-side response latency, and approximately 550 ms total interaction latency under a 350 ms bidirectional network budget.

</details>


### [71] [Weakly-Supervised RGB-D Salient Object Detection via SAM-driven Pseudo Annotation and State Space Interaction-based Diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15041)
*Wenqi Si,Gongyang Li,Shixiang Shi,Weisi Lin*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A scribble-supervised RGB-D salient object detection framework combining SAM-based pseudo annotation generation (SAM-PAG) and a conditional diffusion model (S^2Diff) to expand sparse scribbles into dense annotations and iteratively refine saliency maps, achieving competitive results with fully-supervised methods across seven datasets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address the high annotation cost in RGB-D SOD by leveraging weak scribble supervision; convert sparse scribbles into dense pseudo labels and refine maps via diffusion with strong conditioning.

Method: SAM-PAG expands sparse scribbles to dense pseudo annotations using Segment Anything Model with a dual-branch design and mask-consistency; S^2Diff uses a diffusion model conditioned on cross-modal features obtained through a cross-modal generation module (frequency integration and implicit-explicit state space interaction) and a context injection module to denoise and enhance saliency maps.

Result: Outperforms existing scribble-supervised methods and achieves competitive performance with fully-supervised methods on seven datasets; code available at the provided GitHub link.

Conclusion: The proposed framework effectively leverages SAM to mitigate sparse supervision and a diffusion-based refinement with cross-modal conditioning to produce accurate saliency maps, reducing annotation requirements while maintaining competitive SOD performance.

Abstract: Weakly-supervised RGB-D Salient Object Detection (SOD) is explored to reduce the heavy burden of pixel-level annotations. But scribble annotations lack the structure and details of objects, resulting in inaccurate saliency maps. In this paper, we propose a novel scribble-supervised RGB-D SOD method, consisting of a Segment Anything Model (SAM)-driven pseudo annotation generation method (\emph{SAM-PAG}) and a state space interaction-based conditional diffusion model (\emph{$S^2$Diff}). Specifically, SAM-PAG is tailored to address the issue of sparse supervision information. In SAM-PAG, we adopt the advanced SAM to expand sparse scribbles to dense pixel-level pseudo annotations through the dual-branch structure and the consistency of segmentation masks. In $S^2$Diff, we adopt the diffusion model to iteratively refine the noisy saliency maps with the guidance of conditional information, generating accurate saliency maps. Naturally, the core of our $S^2$Diff lies in the acquisition of conditional features and the denoising of saliency maps. For the former, we employ a cross-modal conditional generation module to interweave cross-modal features through frequency integration and implicit-explicit state space interaction, effectively achieving global conditional features. For the latter, we employ a context injection module to mitigate noise interference and to enhance object information with the conditional context. With the close cooperation of SAM-PAG and $S^2$Diff, our method outperforms relevant scribble-supervised methods and achieves competitive performance compared to fully-supervised methods on seven datasets. The code and results of our method are available at https://github.com/Switch457/WeakS2Diff_SOD.

</details>


### [72] [Parameter-efficient Prompt Tuning of Vision Foundation Model With Adaptive Focal Loss for Interpretable MCI Screening](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15047)
*Javad Khoramdel,Farhad Hoseyni,Amirhossein Nikoofard*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Efficient, explainable MCI detection from neuropsychological drawings using a frozen DINOv2-Small with three prompt tokens; 1.19M trainable params; intrinsic spatial explainability via attention maps; MoCA-adapted focal loss for boundary ambiguity; 5-fold CV yields F1 0.641, AUC 0.795, outperforming ResViT by 0.110 in F1.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address data scarcity, class imbalance, and diagnostic ambiguity at clinical boundaries in automated MCI detection from drawing tests; supply a parameter-efficient model with intrinsic explainability.

Method: Freeze DINOv2-Small; three modality-specific learnable prompt tokens queried in a shared cross-attention layer over image patch tokens; 1.19M trainable params; attention maps provide spatial explainability; task-conditioned embeddings fused via attention to quantify modality importance; MoCA-adapted focal loss incorporating continuous cognitive scores into target, loss modulation, and adaptive sample weighting; 5-fold stratified cross-validation.

Result: MCI-class F1 0.641; AUC 0.795; outperforms ResViT baseline by 0.110 in F1.

Conclusion: Demonstrates that a compact, prompt-tuned, self-attention architecture can achieve strong diagnostic performance with intrinsic explainability and improved boundary handling, offering a computationally efficient alternative to heavier hybrids.

Abstract: Mild Cognitive Impairment is a critical early stage of cognitive decline that frequently precedes Alzheimer's disease, yet its automated detection from neuropsychological drawing tests remains fundamentally constrained by data scarcity, class imbalance, and diagnostic ambiguity near clinical boundaries. Existing methodologies attempt to bypass these constraints using computationally expensive, fully fine-tuned hybrid architectures that relegate spatial explainability to a post-hoc approximation rather than an intrinsic model property. We propose a parameter-efficient framework utilizing frozen DINOv2-Small model adapted via three modality-specific learnable prompt tokens while Operating with 1.19 million trainable parameters, each token serves as a query in a shared cross-attention layer over the source image patch tokens. Crucially, spatial explainability is achieved directly through these attention maps; as a structural consequence of the architecture. Then task-conditioned embeddings fused via an attention module to quantify modality-level importance per subject. To handle boundary ambiguity, a MoCA-adapted focal loss introduced that integrates continuous cognitive scores into the training target, loss modulation, and adaptive sample weighting, strictly generalizing standard soft-label approaches. Under stratified five-fold cross-validation, the proposed architecture yields an MCI-class F1 of 0.641 and an AUC of 0.795, outperforming the computationally heavier ResViT baseline by 0.110 in MCI-class F1.

</details>


### [73] [RoGS: Adaptive Meshgrid Gaussian for Large-Scale Road Surface Mapping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15048)
*Tianchen Deng,Zhiheng Feng,Wenhua Wu,Ziming Li,Siting Zhu,Hesheng Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: ROADGS-T presents a robust, efficient large-scale road surface mapping framework using adaptive meshgrid Gaussian surfels that store color, semantic, and geometric information, with adaptive density in complex regions and a trajectory-consistency-based pose refinement strategy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current mesh-based road surface reconstructions suffer from limited reconstruction quality and high optimization cost in large-scale driving scenarios. There is a need for scalable, accurate road surface maps to support HD mapping, lane-level perception, and automatic road annotation.

Method: Model road surfaces with 2D Gaussian surfels placed on a meshgrid, storing color, semantic, and geometric attributes. This meshgrid Gaussian representation reduces redundancy and better fits thin road surfaces than conventional meshes or 3D Gaussians. Implement a road-structure-aware adaptive meshgrid that densifies surfels in complex regions (lane markings, boundaries, height discontinuities) and coarsens flat areas. Apply a trajectory-consistency-guided pose-robust refinement using local priors from multiple neighboring poses, weighting height regularization by geometric consistency.

Result: Not specified in the abstract.

Conclusion:  ROADGS-T is proposed to improve efficiency and fidelity of large-scale road surface mapping and has potential benefits for HD map generation, lane-level perception, and automatic road annotation.

Abstract: Road surface mapping plays a crucial role in autonomous driving, supporting high-definition map generation, lane-level perception, and automatic road annotation. Recent mesh-based road surface reconstruction methods have shown promising results, but they still suffer from limited reconstruction quality and high optimization cost, especially in large-scale driving scenarios. To address these limitations, we propose ROADGS-T, a robust and efficient large-scale road surface mapping framework based on adaptive meshgrid Gaussian representation. Specifically, we model the road surface by placing 2D Gaussian surfels on a meshgrid, where each surfel explicitly stores color, semantic, and geometric information. Compared with conventional mesh-based representations and 3D Gaussian primitives, the proposed meshgrid Gaussian representation better matches the thin-surface property of roads while significantly reducing redundant primitives and overlap during optimization. To further improve representation efficiency and structural fidelity, we introduce a road-structure-aware adaptive meshgrid strategy, which allocates denser Gaussian surfels to geometrically or semantically complex regions, such as lane markings, road boundaries, and height discontinuities, while maintaining a compact representation in flat road areas. Moreover, instead of relying on a single nearest vehicle pose, we design a trajectory-consistency-guided pose-robust refinement strategy, which estimates local surface priors from multiple neighboring poses and adaptively weights pose-guided height regularization according to their geometric consistency.

</details>


### [74] [Beyond Single Expert: Harmonizing Diverse Visual Priors in MLLMs for Spatial Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15054)
*Xiao Lin,Xiaohu Huang,Kai Han*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: ViPS is a multi-model prior framework for MLLMs that uses an Efficient Prior Proxy and Dynamic Prior Fusion to incorporate diverse visual priors, achieving state-of-the-art results in spatial understanding tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing MLLMs typically rely on priors from a single foundation model, which may underutilize complementary strengths across diverse models. The work argues that different visual priors are complementary for different spatial tasks, and combining them can enhance performance.

Method: Introduce Efficient Prior Proxy to generate multiple foundational priors with low inference overhead. Propose Dynamic Prior Fusion to harmonize and contextually fuse these priors and inject them into MLLMs for spatial understanding.

Result: Extensive experiments show that ViPS harmonizes diverse visual priors and achieves new state-of-the-art performance on multiple complex spatial reasoning and 3D spatial understanding benchmarks.

Conclusion: ViPS demonstrates that multi-model priors can be effectively and efficiently integrated into MLLMs to boost spatial understanding, suggesting a scalable path for leveraging diverse foundation models in embodied and 3D perception tasks.

Abstract: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have demonstrated substantial promise in spatial understanding. Existing works typically incorporate prior knowledge extracted from a pre-trained foundation model to further enhance the spatial awareness of MLLMs. In this paper, we first reveal that when integrating diverse foundation models into MLLMs, different models provide complementary spatial priors that benefit different tasks. Motivated by this, we propose $\textbf{ViPS}$, a novel multi-model prior framework designed to fully unleash the potential of incorporating multiple $\textbf{Vi}$sual $\textbf{P}$riors from diverse models into MLLMs for $\textbf{S}$patial understanding. Specifically, ViPS introduces an Efficient Prior Proxy to generate multiple foundational priors with minimal inference overhead, and a Dynamic Prior Fusion mechanism to achieve harmonious and context-aware prior fusion and injection from the prior proxies. Extensive experiments demonstrate that ViPS successfully harmonizes diverse visual priors, establishing new state-of-the-art performance across multiple complex spatial reasoning and 3D spatial understanding benchmarks. Project page: https://visual-ai.github.io/vips

</details>


### [75] [Towards Hierarchical Structure Understanding of Newspaper Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15082)
*William Mocaër,Solène Tarride,Thomas Constum,Merveilles Agbeti-Messan,Tom Simon,Clément Chatelain,Stéphane Nicolas,Pierrick Tranouez,Sébastien Cretin,Thierry Paquet*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Two complementary approaches for newspaper structure understanding: (1) a modular bottom-up pipeline using YOLO for layout detection, LayoutReader for reading order, and a custom article segmentation algorithm; (2) Tiramisu, a novel end-to-end tiered Transformer that models document hierarchy for section/article separation, block localization, semantic categorization, and reading order, plus a new dataset Finlam La Liberté; results show effective reconstruction of complex hierarchies and strengths of each method for scalable digitization; code for Tiramisu available.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The work targets the challenge of deciphering complex, nested, densely laid-out newspaper pages to enable hierarchical information retrieval and scalable digitization, particularly for historical newspapers.

Method: Two-pronged approach: (a) modular bottom-up pipeline combining state-of-the-art components—YOLO for layout detection, LayoutReader for reading order prediction, and a custom article segmentation algorithm—to maintain robustness and interpretability; (b) Tiramisu (Tiered Transformers for Hierarchical Structure Understanding), an end-to-end Transformer model that explicitly encodes document hierarchy through an iterative tiered process to perform section/article separation, block localization, semantic categorization, and reading order prediction; also includes a synthetic newspaper article generator and a dataset (Finlam La Liberté) for evaluation.

Result: Experimental results demonstrate the ability of both approaches to reconstruct complex newspaper hierarchies. Comparative analysis highlights the strengths and trade-offs: the modular pipeline offers robustness and interpretability with flexible component reuse, while Tiramisu provides end-to-end modeling and potential scalability via parallelized attention; together they advance hierarchical information retrieval in historical newspaper digitization.

Conclusion: Both approaches are complementary: the modular pipeline delivers robust, interpretable performance with adaptable components, whereas Tiramisu delivers a coherent end-to-end framework for hierarchical structure understanding. The new Finlam La Liberté dataset supports evaluation of hierarchical information retrieval in historical newspapers, and the authors release Tiramisu training code and a synthetic data generator to facilitate replication and extension.

Abstract: Understanding newspaper images remains a challenging task due to their complex, nested hierarchical structures and dense, heterogeneous layouts. In this paper, we explore two complementary approaches for newspaper structure understanding. First, we present a modular bottom-up pipeline that combines state-of-the-art open-source models: YOLO for layout detection, LayoutReader for reading order prediction, and a custom algorithm for article segmentation. This approach leverages existing robust components while maintaining flexibility and interpretability. Second, we introduce Tiramisu (Tiered Transformers for Hierarchical Structure Understanding), a novel end-to-end transformer-based architecture that explicitly models document hierarchy through an iterative tiered process. Tiramisu performs section and article separation, block localization, semantic categorization, and reading order prediction using highly parallelized attention mechanisms. Finally, we release Finlam La Liberté, a new dataset designed specifically for evaluating hierarchical information retrieval in historical newspapers. Experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of both approaches in reconstructing complex newspaper hierarchies, with comparative analysis highlighting their respective strengths for scalable document digitization. The Tiramisu training code, including the synthetic newspaper generator, is available at https://git.litislab.fr/tiramisu/tiramisu-newspaper-articles-extractor.

</details>


### [76] [Quantifying Training Membership Information in the Hyperspherical Embedding Geometry of Face Recognition Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15084)
*Ünsal Öztürk,Sébastien Marcel*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Four geometry-based statistics quantify member vs non-member cluster differences in face embeddings; training-identity count dominates separability; cross-domain factors inflate signals; a learned classifier fuses statistics to reveal extra membership information.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify and understand how non-member geometric properties differ from member clusters in face recognition embeddings and how training-time factors affect this geometry, enabling more reliable interpretation of membership signals.

Method: Compute four cluster-geometry statistics on member and non-member embeddings across 180 IResNet-based models in a factorial design varying backbone size, loss head, training duration, and number of training identities; evaluate on nine benchmarks; analyze cross-domain non-member benchmarks; fuse four statistics with a learned classifier.

Result: The number of training identities has the largest effect on member/non-member separability; backbone and loss head contribute far less; in the same-domain reference, the geometric membership signal decreases monotonically as training identities increase; cross-domain non-member benchmarks inflate the apparent membership signal; a learned classifier combining the four statistics reveals additional membership information beyond the best single statistic.

Conclusion: Training-set size is the dominant driver of geometric membership signal in face embeddings; cross-domain biases can distort interpretation; combining multiple geometry statistics via a learned classifier improves membership detection beyond any single statistic; these findings inform evaluation and analysis of embedding-based face recognition systems.

Abstract: Face recognition models represent each face as an embedding vector on the unit hypersphere by clustering embeddings of the same identity while pushing different identities apart through angular-margin losses. Because these losses act only on training identities, non-member identities may form clusters with different geometric properties. In this paper, we quantify the magnitude of this difference and what training-time factors control it. We compute four statistics based on cluster geometry across 180 face recognition models in a factorial design over IResNet backbone size, loss head, training duration, and the number of training identities, and evaluate each configuration on nine benchmarks. Our results indicate that the number of training identities has the largest effect on member/non-member separability, while backbone and loss head contribute far less, and that, on a same-domain held-out reference, the geometric membership signal decreases monotonically as more identities are added to training. We provide an analysis of cross-domain (pose, age, quality, ethnicity) non-member benchmarks and report that these inflate the apparent membership signal. Finally, we fuse all four statistics with a learned classifier to reveal additional membership information beyond the best individual statistic.

</details>


### [77] [AlphaWiSE: Adaptive Weight Interpolation for Continual Multimodal Representation Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15094)
*Sarthak Jain,Qiran Hu,Zhen Zhu,Yaoyao Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: AlphaWiSE is a post-hoc weight-space interpolation method that composes two frozen source checkpoints using a single scalar per aligned tensor, enabling continual adaptation for multimodal models without extra inference cost and with improved cross-modal retrieval.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Continual adaptation can disrupt cross-modal alignment learned in earlier training; traditional continual-learning approaches often fix a single stability-plasticity trade-off. A simple, post-hoc method to flexibly interpolate between source models can preserve past knowledge while adapting to new data.

Method: Identify aligned parameter tensors across the two source checkpoints. For each tensor, fit one scalar interpolation coefficient α_t (shared across all entries of that tensor) using a small exemplar memory.
